# Player Controller & Input

> Authoring guidance: .claude/docs/templates/guidance/game-design-document-guide.md (load per-section as you author — do not read entirely).

> **Status**: Designed
> **Author**: user + game-designer, ux-designer, gameplay-programmer, creative-director, systems-designer, qa-lead (via /design-system, autonomous)
> **Last Updated**: 2026-10-27 (design review: 5 blocking findings and 14 recommended items applied; see the *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review* tags and Open Questions 16, 19–27)
> **Last Verified**: 2026-10-26
> **Creative Director Review (CD-GDD-ALIGN)**: APPROVED 2026-10-26 (after one CONCERNS round: six documentation carry-through items for the chest-mounted lamp and the throw binding; no design change)
> **Implements Pillar**: Dread Before Chaos (P2 — the body's steadiness is what lets the two rhythms be read off the world instead of the controls); The Web Watches (P3 — every step, light and tool use is a stimulus the player owns); The World Never Lies (P1 — the body and its beam are never-shift anchors)

## Summary

The Player Controller & Input is the crewmate's body and hands: it turns keyboard, mouse or gamepad input into the two things the simulation accepts — intents (where I am walking, where my chest lamp points, whether I am running) and commands (cut, weld, pick up, drop, throw a glowstick) — and turns the simulation's answer back into a stiff, heavy, honest body on screen. The body is the same in the quiet boarding rhythm and the frantic breach: no adrenaline, no speed change, nothing that lets a player read the danger off their controls instead of off the world. Movement is whole-pixel integer kinematics with no physics body; the lamp is chest-mounted and hands-free; every acknowledgement is immediate and never encodes success; the sprite, its tools and its beam are anchors a hallucination can never edit. Sixteen systems depend on the intent/command split and the facing, reach and noise rules it fixes.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Core` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer`, `Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera` (both designed); position ownership provisional with `Ship Body & Interior Physics`

## Overview

The Player Controller & Input is the crewmate's body and hands: it turns what the player does at the keyboard, mouse or gamepad into the two kinds of thing the simulation accepts — **intents** (where I am trying to walk, where I am pointing the beam, whether I am running) and **commands** (cut this hatch, toggle the beam, pick that up, drop it, start welding) — and it turns the simulation's answer back into a body on screen: five drawn facings and their mirrors, a stiff-weighted walk and run, a module carried at chest height, a stagger when the air goes bad, the lamp beam from the suit's chest-mounted light at the per-facing chest point. In the simulation it owns the player's intent and facing as a `PER_PLAYER` state block keyed by seat; whether it also owns ship-local position, or Ship Body & Interior Physics does, is the one open boundary it shares with that system (kernel Open Question 3) and is settled in this GDD provisionally.

The player operates this system every second of play, and its whole design goal is that they stop noticing it. It is active in the deliberate boarding rhythm (every step a decision, the beam swept slowly onto a doorway) and in the frantic breach rhythm (running a corridor while the ship fails around them) — and it is **the same body in both**: no adrenaline, no speed buff, no change of handling. The breach is frantic because of what moves in the world, not because the body changes (art bible 2.8).

It exists because the pillars put the player's body on the truth side of the game. *The World Never Lies*: the character sprite, its tools and its beam are never-shift anchors — a crewmate rendered as a horror still carries their true beam, and the body's honest states (rime, fogged visor, stagger) are a cross-check a low-sanity player can trust. *The Web Watches*: every step, every lit beam and every running stride is a stimulus the player owns — running is loud, and the controller is where that cost is paid. *Dread Before Chaos*: a slow, heavy body makes a door a decision. Every downstream system that needs a player to act — Interaction, Suit & Equipment, Modular Ship Assembly, Attention, the camera's follow target, the HUD's hand rail, co-op bodies as mirrored state — depends on the intent/command split this GDD fixes.

**Design-level only.** `CharacterBody2D` vs hand-rolled kinematics on a moving ship, how input actions are read, the animation driver — implementation choices → **becomes an ADR** (ship body / interior physics; input adapter). *(Framing: both the data layer and its player impact — `Core` category says technical, but this is the system the player is; fantasy direct.)*

## Player Fantasy

*Direct — the player operates this system every second.*

**The ship changes tempo; the body does not.** The player is the Sealed Technician: a round helmet on a square pack, stiff, weighted, upright, competent and fragile. The body moves the same way in a silent derelict and in a venting corridor. It walks without bounce, the pack rises and falls because the suit is sealed, and the chest-mounted lamp swivels on its plate independently of the torso: the body snaps between eight facings while the beam sweeps freely through 360°, so the beam follows the player's hand, not the player's shoulders. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier line "the beam turns exactly as fast as the player turns their body" was false by construction. The weight the pillar needs is carried by the stiff cycle, the run ramp, the carry slowdown and an optional walk-start ramp (`WALK_START_TICKS`, D.4), and is judged by the feel protocol in Game Feel; a non-lit swivel cue on the lamp housing is an art ask, Visual/Audio.)* Looking at something means lighting it. The body answers the frame the player presses and the ship confirms a tick later, so the hand never waits for the simulation. In the boarding rhythm that steadiness is patience: a deliberate step, a slow sweep, every door a decision. In the breach rhythm the same steadiness is the one thing the player can still trust. The panic is in the world — red sweeping, loops venting, a trail outrunning you down the corridor — and never in the controls. The player's only levers are their own choices: whether to run, which leans the body forward and is noise the web can hear, and where to point the light.

The anchor is one gesture made twice. The first time is early: the player steps out of the cryo pod, takes a few careful steps and swings the beam onto the doorway out, and the frame comes up out of the black. The second time comes later, with the same doorway or one like it and the alarm sweeping. The player swings the beam at the same speed, through the same arc, with the same hands. The room has changed and the body hasn't, which is how the player knows the danger is out there and not in them. When the body does change, the change is true. A stagger means the suit is failing, and the beam stays on the stumbling body: its origin rides the chest point, while its aim, shape and reach stay as the player and the light made them. Rime on the plates and a fogged visor are real cold and real bad air, never mood.

In co-op, a body tells the truth about its owner. A crewmate who walks like a technician and carries one of the four beam shapes is a crewmate, even through a hallucinated horror rig, because the rig is driven by their real motion and their real beam.

**Pillars served.** *Dread Before Chaos*: "Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived… This pillar owns tempo and presentation." The body's steadiness keeps tempo in the world, where the tell can be read. *The Web Watches*: "The players' light, noise, power use and restraint raise or lower its attention… Restraint and stealth are real strategies." Running and aiming are the restraint choices the player holds every second. *The World Never Lies*: "Only instruments and perception can be wrong." The body is not an instrument, and its vital states are physical consequences. *Sanity Rewrites Reality*, secondary test: "−10% move speed vs. a gauge showing a false value → the false gauge." Sanity lies through the world, never through the legs.

**Design tests.**
- If we are debating whether the body or the beam may do something the player's input did not do and the simulation did not cause (aim snap or assist, auto-vault, auto-step, a camera-led turn, a scripted walk, a contextual animation that moves the character or reorients the beam): it may not. Presenting what happened is permitted: render interpolation, the per-facing chest point (beam origin), quantising facing to 8 directions while the beam rotates 360°, and immediate local acknowledgement.
- The fantasy has failed if the same input, at the same suit vitals and the same carried load, produces different movement or beam motion in the boarding rhythm, in the breach rhythm or at low sanity. That would mean some movement or aim parameter reads Attention, breach state or sanity level. It is a Pillar 2 violation, because the player would be reading the rhythm off their controls instead of off the world — not a tuning issue.

*What this section does not claim*: the controller does not own what light and noise cost (Emitter State and Attention), when rime, fog or stagger appear (the game-designer, via Suit and vitals), the speeds, acceleration and turn rates that make "stiff" responsive (Detailed Design and Game Feel), or the input bindings (ux-designer). The only permitted "shake" of the beam is its *origin* moving with the body — the chest point rides the run lean and the stagger frames (Pandorum's chest lamp); aim angle, cone shape, reach and temperature never change with the body, so body motion stays separable from sanity's light-lies. Whether the stagger's hand-point displacement needs a reduced-motion variant is flagged for `design/accessibility-requirements.md`; per kernel CR-11, presentation easing never changes the sim. *(Alternatives considered: "Looking is spending" — true, but credits the controller with Emitter State's and Attention's fantasy; "The body that cannot lie" — true, but credits the controller with Suit's honesty and risks implying a sanity self-tell, which art bible 5.3 forbids.)*

**Trade-off accepted.** The body never "rises to the occasion": no adrenaline burst, no breach sprint. Barotrauma's tempo, but your own legs. If playtests read the body as sluggish, the fix is base responsiveness for both rhythms, never a breach-only modifier. *We'll know this was right if* playtesters describe breaches as "the ship went crazy," not "my character got weird," and a code review finds no movement or aim value whose inputs include Attention, rhythm state or sanity.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Specialists consulted: game-designer (primary), gameplay-programmer, ux-designer; creative-director for Section B. Disagreements resolved and logged 2026-10-26.*

*Specialists consulted: game-designer (primary), gameplay-programmer (feel, kinematics, presentation contract), ux-designer (bindings, focus model, comfort, rebinding), with systems-designer (Suit thresholds) and creative-director (pillar alignment) as reviewers. Decisions logged 2026-10-26 (autonomous mode). Items tagged **→ ADR** are engine-integration choices deliberately left out of this behaviour spec. Rules are numbered `PC-n` (the kernel owns `CR-n`, the pipeline `C-n`/`PR-n`).*

**Vocabulary.** *Input Adapter*: the presentation-side object that reads devices, quantises, and **submits** commands as the local seat — kernel subscriber class `INPUT_SURFACE` (CR-10). *Controller Sim*: the simulation system that **owns** block `controller.state` (per controlled actor: move intent, gait, committed aim, facing, locomotion state, carry and channel references, chest point and hand point) and runs step hook `controller.step` at divisor 1. *Avatar Presenter*: the `PRESENTATION`-class node that draws any crewmate body (local or remote) from read views; never submits. *LocomotionStep*: Body's pure integer kinematic step (PC-3). *Facing*: one of 8 octants (S, SE, E, NE, N, NW, W, SW), five drawn, three mirrored (art bible 5.6). *Raw aim*: the Adapter's local, un-quantised, per-frame aim; *committed aim*: `controller.state.aim_angle` as last applied. *Aim-live*: the aim source produced input this frame or (mouse) exists at all; *aim-idle*: a released stick. *Gait* ∈ {WALK, RUN}. *Body frame*: the ship-local frame of the body the actor is on (pipeline C2). *Channel*: a multi-tick action (cut, weld, patch) whose progress accrues in its owner's block. *Context*: the Adapter's input context ∈ {`gameplay`, `chart_table`, `inspect_card`, `chat`}.

**PC-1. Three parts, and the control-manifest lines that separate them.** (a) The **Input Adapter** reads devices, quantises, and submits `PLAYER`-origin commands as peer 1 acting on the entity the binding table binds to PlayerId 1 (CR-7). It writes no block, ever; it owns only local state (raw aim, last-acknowledged intents, hold timers, run latch, active device, context, pre-play tokens). It samples input **in the physics callback immediately before `Kernel.step()`**, so an intent submitted in frame N is frozen into tick T's P1 batch, applied in P2 and integrated by Body in P3 of the same tick — one tick of latency, never two. It never requests a Sanity, Attention or `PERCEPTION_OUT` read view: input mapping, dead-zones and aim source are HR-6 anchors and must be structurally unable to lie. (b) The **Controller Sim** registers `controller.state` (`SIM`, `persist = true`, `persist_scope = WORLD`, `scope = PER_PLAYER` keyed by PlayerId, `repl_hint = SHARED_SUMMARY`), command types `set_move_intent` and `set_aim`, and hook `controller.step` (divisor 1, `after: suit.vitals`). It writes its block in its own `apply` handlers, inside its hooks, and through four published **Class B mutators** that other owners' `apply` handlers and hooks call for cross-block writes (CR-9 a): `set_channel_ref(actor, owner_system, channel_id)`, `clear_channel_ref(actor)`, `set_carry_ref(actor, item_id)`, `clear_carry_ref(actor)` (Interaction IA-36; Modular Ship Assembly calls `set_channel_ref(actor, ASSEMBLY, channel_id)` and `clear_carry_ref(actor)`); it may not submit `PLAYER`-origin commands (CR-3). It also registers the `SYSTEM`-origin command type **`stagger {actor}`** (`allowed_origins {SYSTEM}`, `coalesce = NONE`, `max_age_ticks = 120`, no player binding; rejected `NOT_STAGGERABLE` in `DOWNED`, `DEAD` or `IN_POD`; `Applied` in `STAGGERED` without extending the lock, so a stagger never chains into a stun-lock), which Suit & Equipment submits on a two-band pressure drop within `STAGGER_WINDOW_TICKS` (SE-35) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from suit-and-equipment)* (Horror Entities submits none, HE-6; PC-9), and a second divisor-1 hook **`controller.noise`** (`after: [body.integrate]`, D.13). Its `SIM` read view publishes `octant`, `run`, `gait_effective`, `aim_angle`, `aim_live`, `facing`, `loco_state`, `carry_ref`, `channel_ref`, the chest point and the hand point; `loco_state` is the name Attention (AT-31: the living-player and `IN_POD` tests) and Horror (HE-33, C-HE-18) read. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from interaction-system, modular-ship-assembly, suit-and-equipment, ship-body-and-interior-physics, attention-system, horror-entities)* (c) The **Avatar Presenter** reads `body.state` (integer position prev/curr, odometer), `controller.state` (facing, gait, locomotion state, chest point, hand point), `emitter.state` (beam lit, shape) and `tick_alpha`; for the *local* avatar only it also reads the Adapter's raw aim. It writes exactly: frame index, `flip_h`, integer position, overlay visibility, light position and light rotation — **never scale, rotation, skew or modulate on the body** (lint). *Rationale.* "Controller submits via `INPUT_SURFACE` only; never writes blocks" is true of the Adapter and false of the Controller Sim; one name for both would read as "this system has no block", and facing and gait would end up as presentation state that co-op cannot replicate and a save cannot restore. *Alternative rejected:* folding the Controller Sim into Ship Body — intent and facing are seat semantics (what the player asked for, read by Interaction, Attention and the HUD); position is a kinematic fact. Keeping them apart keeps the input record separate from the integrator.

**PC-2. Input model: contexts, actions, intents, commands.** The game reads **named actions** (`StringName`, rebindable), never raw keys and never `device == 0` (4.7: `InputEvent.DEVICE_ID_MOUSE` / `DEVICE_ID_KEYBOARD`; identify devices by event type). **→ ADR** (InputMap vs a custom binding layer; edge detection by event vs polling).

*Contexts.* Exactly one context is active: `gameplay`, `chart_table`, `inspect_card`, `chat`. Entering any non-`gameplay` context sends `set_move_intent {NONE}` and clears the run latch; entering `chart_table` or `chat` also submits `cancel_channel` for a hold- **or** toggle-mode channel (in the co-op overlay the sim keeps running and a channel the player cannot see must not keep burning); entering `inspect_card` does **not** cancel the channel, so the Pillar 4 "second instrument" check on the pipe being welded is free (the zeroed move intent is not a move, the channel continues, `interact` is still tracked for hold mode and a release inside the card still cancels; `cancel` closes the card first and a second `cancel` cancels the channel). Any transition of `loco_state` to `STAGGERED`, `DOWNED`, `DEAD` or `IN_POD` returns the context to `gameplay` on the same frame (the Adapter observes the published state; the card or overlay closes and the cursor is hidden again). Pause is reachable in every state including `DOWNED`: `chart_table` and `request_pause` are Adapter-level and never gated by `loco_state`. The full **context × action matrix** is in the table below. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; supersedes the earlier "cancel all channels on every non-`gameplay` entry" note for `inspect_card` only. Routed to Instruments and Interaction as a cross-GDD note.)* The OS cursor is **hidden and confined** in `gameplay` (HUD HS-52: no cursor in `gameplay`; the beam is the pointer) and **shown and released** in `chart_table` and `chat`; in `inspect_card` it stays **hidden and inert** (HUD HS-52: `set_aim` stays live there, and a visible cursor would be a second pointer over the beam; PC-16). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from hud-and-suit-layer, in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view; cursor visibility in `inspect_card` corrected 2026-10-27 — design review, HS-52 owns it)* Zoom and pause actions are reachable from `gameplay` and `inspect_card`; `chat` (VS-B) swallows text keys and is the only keyboard-only flow in the core loop (HR-14).

*Default bindings (`gameplay` context).*

| Action | KB+M | Gamepad | Kind | What the Adapter submits |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `move_up/down/left/right` | WASD | left stick | continuous | part of `set_move_intent` |
| `run` | Shift | LT | hold (toggle option, PC-3) | part of `set_move_intent` (`run`) |
| aim | mouse | right stick | continuous | `set_aim`; raw aim kept locally |
| `beam_toggle` | F | Y | press | `toggle_beam` (owner: Emitter State) |
| `interact` | E (alias LMB) | A | press / hold | the command type **Interaction declares** for the focused object (PC-6) |
| `cancel` | X | B | press | `cancel_channel` if channelling; else closes the current UI |
| `carry_drop` | G | X | press | `drop` (owner: Interaction) |
| `tool_1..6`, `tool_cycle` | 1–6, Tab | d-pad right / left (next / previous tool) | press | `equip_tool` (owner: Suit & Equipment) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: pad cycle moved from RB / LB to the d-pad so RB can carry `throw`; ux-designer ruling adopted)* |
| `aim_up/down/left/right` | arrow keys | — | continuous | keyboard aim: the four keys compose an 8-way vector exactly like movement (D.6 composition, opposite keys cancel); `aim_q` = the octant centre `512 × o`, `live = true` while any is held; `set_aim` as in D.7 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: makes keyboard-only aim possible; proposed, ux-designer owns the keys)* |
| `aim_hold` | Left Ctrl | — | hold | while held, the four `move_*` keys steer **aim** (as the arrow keys do) and `set_move_intent {NONE}` is sent: stand and look, hands on one block of keys *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; proposed, ux-designer owns)* |
| `zoom_in`, `zoom_out` | wheel up/down, = / − | d-pad up / down | press (edge) | no command — pipeline C6 consumes (PC-16) |
| `inspect` | R (alias RMB) | RT | press | context switch to `inspect_card`; no command — a presentation-only switch (IA-38), not a verb (HR-14 mirror card; ships at MVP, IW-21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view)* |
| `chart_table` | Esc | Start | press | `request_pause` solo (control plane via the menu); overlay only in co-op |
| `throw` | Q | RB | press | `throw_glowstick {aim_angle}` (owner: Interaction; charges: Suit & Equipment) — adopted 2026-10-26, amendment (9); *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: pad binding moved from R3 to RB (R3 pressed the aim stick and collided with the dev chord); ux-designer ruling adopted)* |
| *(unbound)* | — | L3, R3, LB, View | — | reserved for the player's rebinds (dev builds only: L3 + R3 is the Debug Overlay reveal chord, PC-2 dev-only note; the chord no longer shadows any gameplay action) |

The wheel is **zoom**, not tool cycling — the first conflict the per-context rebinding model catches (PC-13).

*Context × action matrix.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: new; the Adapter's policy per context, one row per action family. "ignore" = the press is dropped before submit with no pre-play.)*

| Action family | `gameplay` | `chart_table` | `inspect_card` | `chat` (VS-B) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `move_*`, `run`, `aim_*` keys | live | navigation only (D.6 four-way) | ignore (move zeroed); mouse/stick **aim stays live** (PC-16) | ignore |
| mouse / right-stick aim (`set_aim`) | live | not sent | **live** | not sent |
| `interact` (begin, tap, hold-confirm) | live | accept (chart) | ignore as a new press; a held channel's `interact` is still tracked (hold mode) | ignore |
| channel already running on entry | continues | **cancelled** (`cancel_channel`, both modes) | **continues**; `cancel` closes the card first | **cancelled** |
| `cancel` | `cancel_channel` if channelling, else closes UI | back | closes the card (channel untouched) | closes chat |
| `beam_toggle`, `throw`, `carry_drop`, `tool_*` | live | ignore | ignore | ignore |
| `zoom_in/out`, `chart_table` (pause) | live | `chart_table` closes; zoom ignore | live | zoom ignore |
| `inspect` | opens the card | ignore | closes the card | ignore |
| forced return to `gameplay` | — | on `STAGGERED`, `DOWNED`, `DEAD`, `IN_POD` | on the same four | on the same four |

*Default bindings (`chart_table` context).* No new action. Directional navigation reuses the physical controls of `move_up/down/left/right` (WASD, left stick), which the Chart Table re-reads four-way under `MOVE_DEADZONE` / `MOVE_DEADZONE_EXIT`; accept is `interact` (E / LMB / A); back is `cancel` (X / B); close is `chart_table` (Esc / Start); the wheel scrolls. Conflicts are checked per context (PC-13), so the reuse is legal. **Console seam:** at the pod console the Adapter injects the host-local `ChartPick` into `pod_hop`'s payload; a console press with no pick submits nothing and opens the chart (`chart_table` context) instead — a context switch, not a verb choice (Chart Table ASK-CT-1, ASK-CT-2). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from chart-table-web-map-and-menus)*

*Dev-only actions (dev feature tag only).* The Adapter registers the ten actions of registry `debug_overlay_actions` — `debug_overlay_toggle`, `debug_overlay_page_next`, `debug_overlay_page_prev`, `debug_overlay_mode`, `debug_overlay_dump`, `debug_overlay_reveal`, `debug_overlay_mark`, `debug_overlay_cursor_next`, `debug_overlay_cursor_prev`, `debug_overlay_activate` (placeholder defaults toggle F3, page next F4, page prev Shift+F4, mode Insert, dump Home, reveal Ctrl+Shift+F3 with the pad chord L3 + R3, mark End, cursor next PageDown, cursor prev PageUp, activate Delete; F5-F8 avoided as editor run, pause and stop shortcuts; Debug Overlay DO-8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* — **only under the dev feature tag**, and forwards them host-locally to the Overlay: never a command, never in the binding table Settings sees, never in a rebinding, preset or accessibility enumeration, absent from release. The Adapter is their only `Input` reader (Gaps: the cross-GDD lint collision). **Pad chord:** the dev chord L3 + R3 shadows no gameplay action now that `throw` is on RB; L3 and R3 are unbound by default in every build. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from debug-overlay-true-values; rewritten 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier "R3 consumed by the chord before `throw`" rule is void)*

*Intents vs commands (CR-4).* Two types are **intents**, `coalesce = LATEST_PER_ACTOR_PER_TICK`, `max_age_ticks = 30`: **`set_move_intent {octant: 0..7 | 8 = NONE, run: bool}`** and **`set_aim {aim_angle: int, live: bool}`** in **1/4096-turn units** (`live` is `aim_live`: it travels with the angle because facing rules 4–6 branch on it in the sim). Both intents carry `actor_binding = OWNED_BY_ISSUER`, like commands. Everything else is a **command**: `coalesce = NONE`, `max_age_ticks = 120`, `actor_binding = OWNED_BY_ISSUER`. The Adapter sends `set_move_intent` **only on change** of octant or `run`, with the stick quantised to 8 octants under `MOVE_DEADZONE` = 0.25 and `OCTANT_HYSTERESIS_DEG` = 5°. In one frame it submits `set_move_intent` **before** any command (seq order is the P2 apply order, so a stop and a `begin_*` in the same frame apply in that order, PC-7). It sends `set_aim` when the quantised value differs from `last_acked` by **≥ 1/128 turn (2.8°)**, **or** the aim source changed (mouse ↔ stick ↔ keys), **or** `live` changed, **or** a settle condition holds (inside the threshold but differing at all, for `AIM_SETTLE_TICKS` = 6) — **rate-limited to one per `AIM_SEND_INTERVAL` = 3 ticks**. At rest that is zero sends; a sweep is ≤ 20 sends/s, far inside `MAX_CMDS_PER_TICK`. **No input buffering**: `ACTION_BUFFER_TICKS` = 0; a press during a channel is submitted and answered `BUSY`, honestly; nothing is queued for "after". *Rationale:* buffering hides the sim's answer behind a presentation guess; Zomboid's immediacy is the model. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Zero buffering is a recorded, deliberate trade, not an oversight:** an `interact` for a `CHANNEL` verb pressed one to three ticks before the move keys are up is rejected `MOVING` (PC-7) and costs one honest re-press; the same-tick case (release W and press E in one frame) is solved by ordering, not by buffering (intents are submitted first and validation reads `intent_octant`, PC-7). Adapter-side lever, **off at MVP**: `CHANNEL_BEGIN_GRACE_TICKS` (0–3, default **0**) would hold a `begin_*` press until the Adapter's own last-submitted octant is NONE for at most that many ticks; a playtest finding of "my weld never starts" is the trigger to raise it. *Ignored-input cues (accessibility):* three presses are dropped by Adapter-local knowledge alone and play one short dull cue (`cue_input_ignored`, the same id for all three, no visual, never read from a sim result): `toggle_beam` inside the rate-limit window (PC-5), `interact` with no focused object, and `run` while `carry_ref ≠ none`. The cue is Adapter-local and so cannot reveal a sim answer; `run` under Suit's cap gets none (the cap is Suit's state, shown by the breath and warmth readings).

*Immediate acknowledgement and the pre-play token (CR-4).* On submit the Adapter pre-plays **body and sound only, never the effect**: the hand's switch animation, the tool click, the bar starting. Each pre-play is keyed by the command's `seq` in a `seq → pre-play token` map so a late result cancels the right pre-play. The beam light is the one object that is not pre-played (PC-5). **Rejection classes** (declared by each verb's owner, Interaction's GDD): *immediate* reasons — `OUT_OF_RANGE`, `BUSY`, `INVALID_TARGET`, `ACTOR_NOT_OWNED`, `PAUSED`, every admission reason — **withdraw the ack at once** (the sound stops, the bar vanishes within one frame of the result); the free-lie-detector case of Pillar 4 (a device the player perceives as broken or fixed) is **not a rejection**: the command is `Applied` and the channel resolves to a `PRECONDITION` outcome (Interaction, KRN:96), so the ack is never withdrawn — the work loop and the bar continue until Interaction's channel state signals the outcome. **The ack frame for a command whose channel will complete and one whose channel will resolve to `PRECONDITION` must be pixel- and sound-identical.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from interaction-system)* Rejection is never a shake (C9, HR-13).

*Resync rule (one rule, five triggers).* The Adapter keeps `last_acked` per intent type and must **resend its current quantised value, bypassing the change filter once**, when: (i) an intent result arrives `Rejected(STALE)` (kernel Edge Cases: a stale "stop" otherwise leaves the actor moving); (ii) the kernel leaves `PAUSED` (CR-16 rejects intents immediately while paused, so a release during a pause was lost); (iii) `LOADING → RUNNING`; (iv) window focus returns; (v) **`TRANSFER`**: the actor's `body_of_actor` changes, i.e. a pod arrival (`IN_POD → IDLE`) or a section-detach body transfer, because the new body's first tick must act on the keys physically held *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: pod arrival and body transfer were handled only as an ad-hoc "treated as trigger iii" in Edge Cases)*. For a hold-mode channel it compares the actual button state to the last submitted one on each of those and submits `cancel_channel` if the button is up. **Window focus loss** submits `set_move_intent {NONE}` and `cancel_channel` if channelling, and marks every held action released. Invariant (testable): the sim's intent equals the player's hands within one tick of the kernel being `RUNNING`.

**PC-3. Movement: ship-local, 8-way, two gaits, whole base pixels per tick, no physics body.** Movement is in the **body frame** (pipeline C2); the interior never shows ship motion. Input is **8-way**; gait is the `run` action, never stick deflection. *Rationale:* the noise cost of running (PC-10) must be a deliberate input, not a thumb slip (Sirlin's test for a real decision), and analog speed would be a continuum of non-integer px/tick, which PR-4 rule 3 forbids at camera quantum 1.

*Speeds — derivation.* The pipeline routes "whole base px per tick when the quantum is 1" (PR-4 rule 3) and asserts two build invariants on `v_max`: `LAG_CUT > v_max·τ_follow` → `v_max < 266 px/s`, **and** `0.30·L_h > v_max·τ_follow + L_MAX` at the default field → `0.18·v_max + 72 ≤ 108` → **`v_max < 200 px/s`**. The second binds (the gameplay-programmer's reading and the game-designer's coincide); the brief's 266 is not the operative bound. At `TICK_RATE` = 60 the whole-px/tick ladder is 60 / 120 / 180 / 240 px/s, so the integer ceiling is **3 px/tick = 180 px/s** (lag 32.4 px; 32.4 + 72 = 104.4 ≤ 108). The pipeline's placeholders 90 / 150 px/s are 1.5 / 2.5 px/tick — non-integer under its own routed rule — and are superseded (routed correction).

| Knob | Value | px/s at 60 Hz | tiles/s | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `v_walk` (registry: `V_WALK`) | **2 px/tick** | 120 | 2.5 | Deliberate but not tedious: the 13-tile default field crosses in ≈ 5 s; the boarding rhythm's pace is set by doors and reading. 1 px/tick recorded as the playtest alternative |
| `v_run` (registry: `V_RUN`) | **3 px/tick** | 180 | 3.75 | The integer ceiling under the inner-box invariant; 1.5× walk reads with the forward lean (5.2) and never clamps the camera |
| `v_carry` (registry: `V_CARRY`) | **1 px/tick** | 60 | 1.25 | A module at chest height halves the walk; extraction under rising Attention is the sortie's tension, and the slowdown is *seen* (carry-walk cycle), never a hidden stat |
| `v_max` | = `v_run` | 180 | — | Read by the pipeline's G check |

All speeds are in px/tick and are invalidated by a `TICK_RATE` change (kernel Open Question 12).

*Kinematics (Body's mechanism, this GDD's contract — Body to confirm).* **The avatar has no physics body.** Ship Body runs a hand-rolled integer kinematic step `LocomotionStep.step(state, intent, collide: Callable)` — a pure `RefCounted`, headless-testable: `pos: Vector2i` (base px, body frame) plus `sub: Vector2i` in **1/256-px fixed point**; velocity from an 8-entry integer unit table (cardinal 256, **diagonal 181/256** per axis, so diagonal speed equals cardinal speed to 0.1 %); **axis-separated 1-px AABB sweeps**, x then y every tick, against Layout's integer footprint rectangles, consumed as `SIMULATION` data (walls 24 px deep under the up-screen convention, closed door leaves, props, machinery) — sliding along a wall falls out of axis separation; `sub` **resets to zero on stop**; the foot box is **16 × 8 base px**, horizontally centred on the foot origin, which is the box's top row (`[pos.x − 8, pos.x + 8) × [pos.y, pos.y + 8)`; registry `foot_box`, `FOOT_BOX_W`, `FOOT_BOX_H`). Consequence: the avatar's position is a **pure function of the command stream and the layout**, so the kernel's G3 replay needs no recorded physics observations for crewmates — the Controller/Body pair is deterministic by construction. Body sets `previous := current` in `post_restore` and preserves `sub`, which resets only on the first octant change (registry `move_octant_reset`, `body_state_block`) (pipeline C5). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from ship-body-and-interior-physics, ship-section-and-layout-data-model)* *Why normalised diagonals:* an un-normalised run diagonal (3·√2 = 4.24 px/tick = 254 px/s) would exceed the camera's pan ceiling and cut on every diagonal sprint, and it is the classic diagonal-sprint exploit. *Alternatives rejected:* `CharacterBody2D` (non-deterministic under G3, and the moving composite ship makes the engine's moving-platform handling the risk the systems index names); integer diagonal pairs ((1,1) is 71 % of walk, (2,2) is 141 %).

*Acceleration, stopping, turning.* Start: `RUN_RAMP_TICKS` = 6 — a run begins at `v_walk` for 6 ticks, then `v_run` (integer-only; escape is never blocked because the body is already moving at walk speed on tick 1). Walk start is instant by default; `WALK_START_TICKS` (0–6, default **0**, D.4) is a weight lever that makes the first N ticks of a walk move at 1 px/tick, a rhythm-independent response to the "heavy, not sluggish" question *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; off until the feel protocol in Game Feel asks for it)*. Stop: `STOP_DECEL_TICKS` = 0 (range 0–3, integer steps only). Facing change: instant, mid-cycle (PC-4). Positions are integer base px at commit, so a stop is always on the pixel grid; **no tile snapping** on stop (an uncaused motion the player did not make; C-R's "never reverses unless the player did" extends to the body). No actor pushes another at MVP.

*Run latch (toggle option).* With run as a toggle, the latch is set by a press and **auto-clears** after `RUN_LATCH_CLEAR` = 30 ticks of `octant == NONE`, on channel start, and on entering any non-`gameplay` context — so a latched run never survives a stop the player did not re-decide.

**PC-4. Facing: five drawn, three mirrored; derived from committed aim when the aim is live.** Facing is recomputed **every tick from committed state, with no easing, mid-cycle**, using a **2-quanta sector hysteresis** (2 × 1/128 turn = 5.6°) at octant boundaries. Priority order:
1. **Staggered / downed / dead**: facing frozen.
2. **Channelling**: facing locked to the target's octant (the work loop is two-handed and tool-forward, 5.2); the beam still follows aim from the locked chest point.
3. **Running**: facing = movement octant regardless of aim. *Rationale:* the run cycle is a forward lean of the whole figure; a lean sideways to its motion reads as the horror's asymmetry (3.2 negation rule). The beam is engine light rotating 360° from the chest point (5.6), so a runner's light may point behind them — honest. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier "and tense" claim is struck. No rule currently makes retreating at full run with the beam on the pursuer cost anything but run noise, and the camera look-ahead even shows what is behind; whether to restore a cost is a creative call, **USER DECISION** in Open Question 19.)*
4. **Aim-live** (mouse always; stick deflected): facing = aim octant, whether walking or stationary and **whether or not the beam is lit** — mouse players face the cursor and back-pedal. *Rationale (ux-designer, adopted):* the camera already leans into the aim regardless of beam state (pipeline C4); body and camera must agree, or a beam toggle would turn the body without turning the frame.
5. **Aim-idle, moving**: facing = movement octant.
6. **Aim-idle, stationary**: facing holds.

Back-pedal and side-step use the single walk cycle; when `dot(move, facing) < 0` the Presenter plays the walk cycle **reversed** (zero extra frames). Back-pedal speed is full `v_walk` (`BACKPEDAL_FACTOR` = 1.0; any non-integer value breaks whole-px cadence, so a penalty, if ever wanted, is "walk only" or an integer). Mirroring: SW, W, NW are the SE, E, NE sheets x-flipped (`flip_h`); the hand point's x is negated and the tool changes hands (accepted, 5.6); the chest point barely moves (`|x| ≤ 2`). *Alternative rejected:* the earlier draft's "facing from aim only while the beam is lit" — body and camera would disagree whenever the beam is off.

**PC-5. Aim and the beam.** *Sources.* Mouse: raw aim = direction from the **aim origin** `AIM_ORIGIN = floor(pos_curr) + (0, AIM_ORIGIN_Y)` (a fixed reference chest height, independent of facing, sheet and animation row; default `AIM_ORIGIN_Y` = −35) to the cursor's **look-ahead-free world point** `cursor_aim_world = f_q + (cursor_vp − picture_centre) / k_eff`, where `f_q` is the pipeline's published follow pose **before** look-ahead, the inner-box clamp and jolt (pipeline D.6's `f`, floored to the camera quantum), `cursor_vp` the cursor in the picture and `k_eff` the world scale; inside `AIM_MOUSE_DEADZONE_PX` = 8 base px of the aim origin the aim holds. **Why not C11's `cursor_world`:** C11 maps through `pose_q`, which includes the look-ahead `L`, and `L` is a function of this very aim; the loop (aim moves camera, camera moves the world point under a still cursor, which moves aim) would slew the beam towards a near cursor and keep it drifting after the mouse stops, with no cursor drawn to explain it. Measuring against `f_q` breaks the loop: `f_q` depends on the avatar's motion only, so a still cursor gives a constant `aim_q` while the look-ahead settles, and the beam still goes where the hidden cursor is *relative to the avatar*. The Adapter reads exactly two presentation values from the pipeline for this: `f_q` and the screen→picture mapping (`picture_centre`, `k_eff`); it reads no sim block for it beyond `body.state.pos_curr`. The beam is **drawn** from the real `CHEST_POINT` (PC-5 below); only the *measurement* uses the fixed reference, so a facing flip (the chest point moves up to 4 px horizontally, more vertically across rows) can never move the aim, and the sheet/flip/beam limit cycle with a near cursor cannot occur. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: loop break and facing-independent aim origin; the 2026-10-26 user decision "mouse aim is the direction from the floored chest point to the cursor" is honoured as the **reference chest** — a presentation-neutral measurement point that does not change what the player sees or does; the beam origin is still the true chest point.)* Gamepad right stick: **radial dead-zone `AIM_DEADZONE` = 0.20 (0.10–0.35), scaled-radial, outer `AIM_OUTER` = 0.90, curve exponent `AIM_CURVE` = 1.0; no magnetism, no snap, no slow-down near targets, no input-side smoothing by default**. Keyboard: the arrow keys and `aim_hold` give octant-centre aim (PC-2). **Released-stick behaviour is one setting with three states, `aim_release_mode` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: replaces the binary `aim_follows_movement` knob; folds amendment (9))*:**

| `aim_release_mode` | A released stick (aim-idle) … | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| `FOLLOW_MOVE_WHEN_RELEASED` | slews towards the movement octant centre at `AIM_FOLLOW_SLEW` (default 64 units/tick = 5.6°/tick, so a 180° swing takes 32 ticks; the lamp never whips and never stays stuck on the last door); holds when standing still; any right-stick deflection takes over at once | candidate pad default |
| `HOLD` | holds the last aim forever; until the **first** right-stick deflection of the session the aim follows the movement octant centre (amendment (9): so a pad player who never aims is not stuck pointing south) | **placeholder default** (= the previously decided amendment (9); **USER DECISION** pending, Open Question 21) |
| `FOLLOW_ALWAYS` | the stick is never live; `aim_angle` = movement octant centre on every octant change (one `set_aim` per change); standing still holds; mouse motion takes over | the one-handed preset |

In all three modes a deflected stick is absolute-direction aim and wins immediately; `live` is false whenever the stick is released or in `FOLLOW_ALWAYS`. *Two consumers.* The **raw aim** is read locally by the Avatar Presenter (the local beam's `Light2D` rotation, continuous, in the same frame — lights may rotate off-grid, PR-4 rule 5) and by the camera look-ahead (C4, regardless of lit state); the look-ahead consumes the *angle* of the raw aim measured against `f_q`, so it feeds on a quantity it cannot move *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The **committed aim** (`aim_angle`, 1/4096 turn, ≤ 1 tick late, ≥ 1/128-turn send threshold) is what Emitter State orients the beam emitter by for the exposure query, what Interaction's focus weighting uses, what facing derives from, and what co-op replicates. A ≤ 2.8° / ≤ 1-tick divergence between the drawn and simulated cone is accepted; the identity channel (Pillar 1) is shape and colour, which come from the replicated loadout, never from aim.

*Beam shapes are a loadout, not an input.* The four shapes (wide, narrow, split, fan — art bible 3.2, 8.3) are a per-player Suit & Equipment fact; there is no "change shape" action. `beam_toggle` submits **`toggle_beam`** (owner: Emitter State; actor = the seat's beam emitter ID). The beam is an emitter like any other for Attention, which is what makes "go dark" a strategy.

*Acknowledgement vs the anchor.* The beam is a human-channel anchor that may never lie. If the local light lit on the press and the sim then rejected (`CHARGE_EMPTY`), the beam would flicker once on the one object that may not. Rule: **the toggle's sound and the hand's switch animation pre-play immediately; the light itself changes only on committed state** (≤ 17 ms later, imperceptible against a 3-screen-px cone edge). The Avatar Presenter never predicts beam state. **Pose and value split (LO-2):** the Presenter publishes the local beam pose — position and rotation only — and 2D Lighting & Occlusion writes the value members (`enabled`, `energy`, `texture`, `color`) from `emitter.state`; the Presenter's pose write precedes Lighting's apply in the render callback. **Toggle rate limit (accessibility, WCAG 2.3.1):** the Adapter submits no `toggle_beam` within `BEAM_TOGGLE_MIN_INTERVAL_TICKS` = 10 ticks of its previous submitted `toggle_beam`; a press inside the window is dropped (no pre-play, no click, nothing submitted), so input can flip the beam at most six times (three flashes) per second. The 10-tick floor also covers Suit's six-tick (`GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`) sampled-drain window (SE-35). A sim-side guard in `toggle_beam.validate` is Emitter State's to add (Open Questions). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from 2d-lighting-and-occlusion, emitter-state-and-light-exposure-query, suit-and-equipment)*

*Chest point (beam origin) and hand point (tool grip).* The beam's origin is `floor(interp_pos) + CHEST_POINT[facing][animation]`, mirrored as `(−x, y)` for the three mirrored facings, using the same floored position as the sprite (PR-4 rule 2) so light origin and sprite never disagree; the table is per facing **and per animation** (run lean, carry and stagger frames move it slightly), and art keeps `|x| ≤ 2 px` so a mirror barely moves the light (registry `chest_point`; user decision 2026-10-26 — chest-mounted, hands-free lamp). `HAND_POINT[facing][animation]` is the separate tool-grip anchor and carries no light. The Controller Sim publishes both points in its read view so that when the Perception Filter substitutes a horror rig (class c, art bible 5.0) the beam still originates at the *true* chest point. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check)*

**PC-6. One focus model, interaction reach, lit-only actions, hold-to-confirm.** *Reach* is a sector from the foot origin: `REACH_PX` = 72 base px (1.5 tiles — the adjacent ring including diagonals at 68 px), half-angle `REACH_HALF_ANGLE_DEG` = 70° about the **facing**. Objects behind the player are never candidates. *Focus — the same model on every device:* among interactables that are in reach, in the arc and **lit** (Emitter State's exposure query above the technical-artist's threshold), the focused one minimises `score = d / REACH_PX + FOCUS_AIM_WEIGHT × θ / REACH_HALF_ANGLE_DEG`, with `θ` the angle between the object's direction from the foot origin and the **ground-projected aim** (mouse: the direction from the foot origin to `cursor_aim_world`, PC-5; stick and keys: the committed aim, a direction only) and `FOCUS_AIM_WEIGHT` = 1.0 (0.5–2.0), `FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN` = 0.10 (D.9) — **aim-weighted proximity** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: measuring θ from the chest-height aim origin made a floor object the player points straight at read 30° off; the focus score is Adapter-side presentation, so the ground projection changes no sim value, and `in_reach` is untouched)*; the mouse has no separate pick-under-cursor path (the cursor moves the aim, and aim weights the score, so the model is one). Exactly one object is focused; the HUD draws its prompt (`E · Cut`) only when lit (art bible 7.0), at `world_to_hud(target)`; an off-screen object has no prompt (C9). **The Adapter never chooses the verb**: `interact` is one button, and **Interaction declares, per focused object and equipped tool, the command type and its mode** ∈ {`TAP`, `HOLD_CONFIRM`, `CHANNEL`}; the Adapter applies the matching policy over the same commands. **Decision (art bible 7.0 leaves it to the game-designer): an action is possible only on a lit object.** Interaction's `validate` calls the exposure query the prompt uses, so the prompt never lies by omission. *Rationale:* "what you light, you can read" must also be "what you light, you can touch", or working in the dark becomes a free Attention dodge and "go dark" degenerates into the dominant strategy; light has to cost Attention for darkness to be a trade. Exceptions are Interaction's `act_gate` exempt rows (registry `act_gate`): `drop` of the carried module, `toggle_beam`, the inspect card of an already-lit instrument and `throw_glowstick`. **Known defect, not closed (Open Question 16):** the self-patch (`begin_patch` on object class `ACTOR_SELF`, Interaction IA-17) is the only MVP way to repair the suit (Suit SE-22) and, as the three GDDs stand, can never pass the lit check: Emitter State EM-21 adds the owner's own beam to the owner's `lux_pm` only and states it "is not added to the owner's `act_lux_pm`" (the player is never the target of their own verb), the `lit` flag is computed from `act_lux_pm`, and Interaction lists `NOT_LIT` for `begin_patch`. A player alone with the lamp on gets `NOT_LIT`. This breaks "what you light, you can touch" for the one verb that is about the player's own body. The earlier reading that EM-18/EM-21 already close the question was a misreading. Two fixes, Interaction's and Emitter State's to choose: **(a)** add `ACTOR_SELF` verbs as an `act_gate` exempt row beside `drop`, `toggle_beam` and `throw_glowstick` (smaller; keeps EM-21's "never the target of your own verb" intact everywhere else; this GDD's lean), or **(b)** amend EM-21 so the owner's apex contribution also feeds `act_lux_pm` for `ACTOR_SELF` verbs only. Either way the acceptance criterion in Acceptance Criteria is: lamp on, `begin_patch` on `ACTOR_SELF` is `Applied`; the lamp-off result follows the ruling (under (a) `Applied`, under (b) `NOT_LIT`). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Web Map & Transit's request to exempt `pod_hop` from the lit conjunct (ASK-I2) is Interaction's to accept in `act_gate`; `in_reach` (D.9) keeps its lit conjunct. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from web-map-and-transit)* *Hold-to-confirm* (`HOLD_CONFIRM` mode — destructive verbs, HR-14): a **local** hold of `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` = 0.8 s (0.6–1.2) in render time, drawn on the art bible 7.3 **linear bar — never a ring** (routed correction to the kernel's "progress ring" wording); **release drains the bar at 2×**; on completion one command is submitted; the sim never sees a partial hold. Option: **tap-to-confirm** — two taps, arm then confirm, within `CONFIRM_TAP_WINDOW` = 3 s. No QTEs anywhere. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; D.10 has the arithmetic.)* **Hardening:** (i) each frame adds at most `CONFIRM_DT_MAX_S` = 0.05 s to the bar, so a single 1 s hitch cannot confirm a destructive cut; (ii) the target is **latched** while the bar is above zero: a focus change during a hold neither transfers nor completes the bar, it drains at 2× on the latched target and the new focus starts from zero after release; (iii) modes are exclusive per declaration (IA-8): a `HOLD_CONFIRM` verb submits one `TAP`-class command on completion and never a `begin_*`; if a future verb needs confirm-then-work, Interaction declares it as a `CHANNEL` with a confirm step, the completed bar submits `begin_<verb>`, the plate resets to zero and the HUD re-fills it from the channel's committed progress, and the held `interact` then sustains the channel in hold mode (the "release before re-arm" rule belongs to the confirm bar only); (iv) the tap-window timer is frozen while the kernel is `PAUSED` and disarms on context change.

**PC-7. Channels (cut / weld / patch).** `interact` on an object whose declared mode is `CHANNEL` → the Adapter submits `begin_<verb> {target}` (`coalesce = NONE`; owner Interaction, Assembly behind it for weld/cut). The owner's `validate` requires: lit and in reach, **`controller.state.intent_octant == NONE`**, not carrying, not staggered/downed. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `intent_octant` and `intent_run` are written by the `set_move_intent` `apply` handler in P2; `octant` is the value `controller.step` publishes in P3 and the channel owners' hooks read. Validation that read `octant` saw last tick's value, so releasing W and pressing E in one frame was rejected, and pressing W and E together was accepted and then aborted silently. With the split the first applies and the second is rejected at once with reason `MOVING` (immediate class, "no" cue; proposed to Interaction, today folded into `BUSY` — Open Question 25). Both rely on the Adapter submitting intents before commands in a frame (PC-2).)* **Progress is sim state in `interaction.state`** (with `owner_system` ASSEMBLY for weld and section cut), accrued per tick by Interaction's hook; the owner's `apply` records `channel_ref` through the Controller's `set_channel_ref` mutator (PC-1 b) and the Controller locks facing (PC-4 rule 2). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from modular-ship-assembly, interaction-system)* **Hold is the default; a toggle option exists; both are Adapter policy over the same two commands.** *Interrupts:* (i) **release** (hold mode) or a second press / `cancel` (toggle mode) → `cancel_channel`; (ii) **movement** — the owner's hook reads `controller.state.octant` and aborts when it leaves `NONE` (sim-side, so identical in `MIRROR`; the 8-way dead-zone already filters stick drift); (iii) **stagger / downed** — the Controller Sim clears `channel_ref`; (iv) **target invalidated** (power drops mid-cut, the section detaches) — the owner aborts and reports through state change, never through the command result (CR-5); (v) **unlit** — the owner re-checks the target's `lit` every `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` (6, Interaction IA-7) and aborts `UNLIT`; **proposed grace** (accessibility; Interaction's to adopt, Open Question 25): `LIT_ABORT_GRACE_TICKS` = 30 (0–60, extendable by an accessibility setting) is a counter to which each consecutive dark check adds `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` (so 30 = five consecutive dark checks) and a lit check resets, so tremor, stick drift or sliding off a narrow beam edge no longer aborts a 360-tick weld while a lamp that is switched off still aborts within half a second *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; (vi) a body transfer, a restore, and entry to `chart_table` or `chat` (Contexts, PC-2). The player keeps control of the body at all times: a channel never locks movement, movement cancels the channel. *Rationale:* Dread Before Chaos needs "I can leave now" to be true in every state. **Pause:** the tick stops, progress freezes; on resume the hold survives if the button is still down (PC-2 resync). Whether partial progress persists on the target after an abort is **Interaction's rule** (IA-16). Channel feedback (sounds → audio-director): *start* (pre-play: hand animation + tool start sound + bar appears), *confirmed* (bar begins filling from committed state), *progress* (work loop + tool loop sound), *cancel* (release: bar drains at 2×, tool stops), *abort* (owner-driven: bar snaps empty, a distinct interrupted cue), *completion* (bar fills, completion cue, work loop ends), *rejection* (immediate class: ack withdrawn within a frame, a dull "no" cue); a free-lie-detector channel is never a rejection — it is identical to *confirmed* until it resolves to its `PRECONDITION` outcome (KRN:96). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from interaction-system)* "Stopped", "interrupted" and "nothing happened" are three different sounds.

**PC-8. Carrying.** `pickup` (Interaction) sets `controller.state.carry_ref` through `set_carry_ref`; `carry_drop` → `drop` clears it through `clear_carry_ref` (PC-1 b; Assembly also calls `clear_carry_ref` on an install). While carrying: gait forced to `v_carry` (**no run while carrying** — no carry-run cycle exists and a sprinting module is the wrong image); every `interact` mode except `drop` and the declared carry-admitted rows — `install_module` (`interact` on a HARDPOINT subject whose `compatible_defs(h)` lists the carried instance's def) and `pod_hop` on the CONSOLE — is rejected `BUSY` (hands full); `begin_patch`, `begin_cut`, `begin_weld` and `begin_seam_cut` stay `BUSY`; the beam **stays available** (user decision 2026-10-26 — chest lamp; the carry frames keep the chest point and the forward beam arc clear; overhead at MVP (art-director, AD-BS-1)). *Playtest lever only (the 2026-10-26 user decision — beam available — stands):* beam **off** while carrying — extraction in the dark — is a strong Pillar 3 lever that changes the sortie's difficulty curve more than any other single rule here. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from modular-ship-assembly, boarding-and-salvage, interaction-system)* Stagger does **not** drop the module; downed does.

**PC-9. Honest body states: stagger, downed, dead — driven by the sim, never by a stat.** The Controller Sim reads one value from Suit & Equipment's view (`after: suit.vitals`, divisor 6 — a read up to 5 ticks old is tolerated): **`locomotion_cap` ∈ {FULL, WALK_ONLY, NONE}**, which Suit derives from O₂, temperature and suit integrity (its thresholds; its internal vitals ladder is not read here) — block `suit.state`, field `locomotion_cap`: FULL while the worst rung ≤ 1, WALK_ONLY at rung 2 (CRITICAL), NONE at rung 3 (FAILED) (registry `vitals_ladder_step`; SE-16 to SE-19, SE-24). Rules: `FULL` — no effect (rime and visor fog are Suit-driven overlays the Presenter shows; information, not penalties). **Edge detection is against a stored value, `controller.state.cap_seen`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the edge needs a previous cap and none was stored, so behaviour after a restore while the cap was `WALK_ONLY` was undefined: staggered on every load or never)*: each tick `controller.step` reads the cap, compares it with `cap_seen`, acts on the edge, then writes `cap_seen := cap` — **in every state, including `DOWNED`, `DEAD` and `IN_POD`**, so no edge is ever remembered across a state that ignored it. `cap_seen` is persisted (PC-17); `post_restore` re-reads the cap and sets `cap_seen := cap`, so **a restore never produces an edge** (restoring with the cap at `WALK_ONLY` does not stagger). A cap edge while already `STAGGERED` neither restarts nor extends the timer (`stagger_end_tick` keeps its value; it still updates `cap_seen`). Worked sequence `FULL → NONE → WALK_ONLY → FULL → WALK_ONLY`: `NONE` is `DOWNED` with no stagger; `NONE → WALK_ONLY` is a rising edge (`DOWNED → IDLE`, no stagger); `WALK_ONLY → FULL` is nothing; the final `FULL → WALK_ONLY` is the only falling edge: exactly **one** stagger. **Falling edge `FULL → WALK_ONLY`** → one **stagger** (`STAGGER_TICKS` = 30; input ignored; channel cleared; no displacement — a stagger is animation plus input lock, never an impulse), then **walk only** while the cap holds (the `run` action does nothing; the HUD breath/warmth readings and the visor fog are the readable reason — no banner). **`NONE`** → **downed** (down 6, input ignored, carry dropped); in MVP solo, downed → **dead** after `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` = 1800 unless the cap rises *(a ≈ 30 s wait with no choice before the restart from the last autosave is the slowest recovery attached to the game's commonest failure; shortening it for solo, or adding a "give up and reload" action while downed, is a design call — **USER DECISION**, Open Question 20; 1800 stays as the placeholder and as the VS-B revive window)*; dead hands the actor to Save/Load's MVP death stub (`request_restore` — the controller never calls the control plane). In VS-B a crewmate's revive verb (Interaction) returns downed → idle. External staggers (a venting loop's recoil, art bible 5.2; Suit's two-band pressure drop within `STAGGER_WINDOW_TICKS`, SE-35) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from suit-and-equipment)* arrive as the registered `SYSTEM`-origin `stagger` command (PC-1 b) from the owning system at T+1 (CR-9 path b); Suit is the only MVP submitter (Horror submits none, HE-6; a horror strike reaches the seat as Suit's cap falling edge). **Dropped intents, classified** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: in `STAGGERED`, `DOWNED`, `DEAD` and `IN_POD` a `set_move_intent` is always `Applied` and recorded in `intent_octant` / `intent_run` (so "the sim's intent equals the hands" holds and nothing needs a resync after the lock), and only `v_eff` is forced to 0; a `set_aim` is `Applied` and recorded in `STAGGERED` and `IN_POD` (the lamp keeps following the hand; facing stays frozen in `STAGGERED`), but in `DOWNED` and `DEAD` it is `Applied` as a **no-op** (the angle is not stored) counted by `PC_INPUT_IGNORED{state}`; commands are validated normally and rejected `BUSY` where Interaction says so (drop, throw, begin_*). None of these is ever `Rejected` for state, because a rejection reason would be a second, unprompted channel; ignoring is silent in the sim and visible in the body (the stagger and down sheets). While `DOWNED` or `DEAD` the actor's `set_aim` intents are ignored with the rest of its input, so a downed crewmate's beam holds its last committed aim — the locator a crewmate reads (Lighting E-I). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from suit-and-equipment, 2d-lighting-and-occlusion, horror-entities)* *Why no continuous speed penalty:* the concept's secondary design test (−10 % move speed vs a false gauge → the gauge) is explicit that invisible stat debuffs are the wrong tool; a state the body *shows* — fog, rime, stagger, walk-only — is a channel of truth a crewmate can read in their beam (5.3).

**PC-10. The two rhythms in the controller.** Nothing in this system changes between boarding, the Tell and breach: no adrenaline, no speed buff, no reduced noise, no "panic" input changes. **The breach is frantic because of what moves, not because the body changes** (art bible 2.8). Running is allowed everywhere and is **noisy**: the hook `controller.noise` (divisor 1, `after: [body.integrate]`, D.13) calls Attention's `push_stimulus(NOISE, actor, class)` once on each tick the actor moved, with `class` = WALK, RUN or CARRY from `loco_state`; the class magnitudes are this GDD's gait table `NOISE_WALK` = 1, `NOISE_RUN` = 4, `NOISE_CARRY` = 1 (registry: `NOISE_PER_TICK`; Attention units, 1 AU = one walking tick). Running is 1.5× faster and 4× louder per tick, ≈ 2.7× louder per tile: running never dominates walking for stealth and is a real trade in a retreat. The player's **own running footsteps are audible and distinct from walking** — the only "meter" for noise (audio-director; captions name "your running" for the accessibility-specialist). Tool noise is pushed by Interaction (classes TOOL / LOUD); Suit pushes none at MVP; beam exposure is Emitter State's. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from attention-system, ship-body-and-interior-physics)*

**PC-11. Animation is distance-driven; the human channel; the controller never renders.** Body accumulates an integer **`odometer`** from pixels actually moved post-collision (an actor walking into a wall does not animate); the Presenter's frame is `floor(odometer / stride) mod 8` with `STRIDE_WALK` = 10, `STRIDE_RUN` = 15, `STRIDE_CARRY` = 5 base px — 12 fps at every gait. Idle, work, stagger and down are time-stepped at 12 fps from committed tick counts. The crewmate sprite and its beam are inviolable anchors (Pillar 1; art bible 5.0). This system draws nothing: the Presenter reads committed state; the Perception Filter may substitute the whole body for a horror rig (5.0) but never edits pixels, moves the chest or hand point or touches the beam. Remote crewmates are drawn by the same Presenter from `MIRROR` state (PC-17). Haptics are driven by P1 physical events only (HR-6).

**PC-12. What the player cannot do.** No jump, dodge-roll, vault, lean/peek (C9), melee or attack input (anti-pillar: not a combat game — the friendly-harm act is an Interaction verb on a crewmate target under Sanity's gate, reached through `interact`, never a dedicated button), no auto-aim, magnetism or aim assist of any kind, no acrobatics, no QTEs, no sprint-by-deflection. **No crouch (decided).** *Rationale:* restraint is *light and noise* (Pillar 3), not posture; a quieter crouch is a free dodge every player would hold permanently (Sirlin: a dominant non-decision); the sheet budget has no crouch set; a curled crewmate drifts toward the horror grammar 5.2 forbids. Walking with the beam off *is* the quiet stance.

**PC-13. Devices, switching, rebinding.** **Glyphs follow the last button/key device** with `DEVICE_SWITCH_HYSTERESIS` = 250 ms; **aim source follows the last aim axis** independently; switching is silent, ≤ 1 frame, and **mixed input is permitted** (pad movement with mouse aim). The HR-11 focus bracket shows on key/pad navigation and hides on mouse motion. Godot merges devices per action, so an `interact` held on a pad survives touching the mouse. Switching aim source never jumps the beam (a stick release holds; a mouse move takes over). *Rebinding model* (MVP as data, Alpha as the screen): every action is bindable per device; conflicts are detected **per context** and never silently overwritten; no profile can lack a path to the chart table; presets include **one-handed** (`aim_release_mode = FOLLOW_ALWAYS`, run toggle on, channel toggle on, tap-to-confirm on). Every flow in the core loop is **gamepad-complete** (HR-14); text chat (VS-B) is the only keyboard-only item.

**PC-14. Position ownership — adopted (kernel Open Question 3 / pipeline Open Question 3 closed against Ship Body; technical-director ratification pending).** **Adopted: Ship Body owns ship-local integer position (`pos`, `sub`, `odometer`, `LocomotionStep`); the Controller Sim owns intent, gait, committed aim, facing, locomotion state, carry and channel references, chest point and hand point.** Contract: `controller.step` writes `octant`, `gait_effective`, `facing`, `loco_state`; `body.integrate` (divisor 1, `after: [controller.step, layout.doors]`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from ship-body-and-interior-physics, ship-section-and-layout-data-model)* runs `LocomotionStep` with `collide` bound to Layout's integer footprint rectangles (`SIMULATION` data) and commits `pos_prev / pos_curr`, `odometer`, `blocked`. *Why Body:* the composite welded ship is Body's (systems index high-risk row); an actor transfers between bodies (pod hop, detach) which Body tracks; and with no physics body the step is deterministic, so G3 needs no physics observations for crewmates. *Why not Controller:* it would put the Layout projection inside an input system. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Adopted by Ship Body & Interior Physics** (designed: its "Who owns what" section, BDY-7, BDY-9, BDY-10 take this split verbatim, and the foot box 16 × 8, the 1/256 fixed point and `trunc` rounding are Body's confirmed values); only the technical-director's ratification in the weld spike remains. Body's persisted `last_octant` (the octant its last step integrated) is a different field from this block's `last_octant` (the D.5 decel-ladder memory); both names are kept and the distinction is stated in PC-17.

**PC-15. Hook order and timing.** `controller.step` (divisor 1, `after: suit.vitals`) → `body.integrate` (divisor 1, `after: [controller.step, layout.doors]`; owner Ship Body, BDY-8) → `controller.noise` (divisor 1, `after: [body.integrate]`; reads Body's derived `moved(actor_id)` and pushes the gait class, D.13) and `emitter.place` (divisor 1, `after: [body.integrate, graph.propagate.failure]`; beam emitter at the chest point for the exposure query) → Attention (divisor 6) integrates the pushed noise; a push landing after `attention.step` in a tick integrates on its next run (registry `push_stimulus`; Attention may add `controller.noise` to its own `after` list). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from ship-body-and-interior-physics, ship-section-and-layout-data-model, emitter-state-and-light-exposure-query, attention-system)* Input-to-effect: sampled before `Kernel.step()`, applied P2, integrated P3, drawn next render (≤ 17 ms + render latency).

**PC-16. Inputs owned here, consumed elsewhere.** `zoom_in` / `zoom_out` are two edge-triggered player inputs (pipeline C1) consumed by the pipeline's zoom ladder; `chart_table` calls `request_pause` (CR-15) through the menu in single-player and opens an overlay with the kernel `RUNNING` in co-op (CR-16); `inspect` enters the `inspect_card` context (the Adapter is the `INPUT_SURFACE` and owns the context; it publishes a presentation-local `{context, inspect_ref}` signal that In-World Instruments, a `PRESENTATION` surface, subscribe to — registry `inspect_card_context_signal`; never a command). The inspect card ships live at MVP (In-World Instruments IW-21). In `inspect_card`, `set_aim` stays live (the Adapter keeps sampling aim and sending `set_aim` as in `gameplay`), so the lamp can hold the gauge lit; the OS cursor stays hidden there (HUD HS-52; the mouse still steers the aim and picks nothing on the card, Instruments K-14) *(corrected 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view)* In `PAUSED` the Adapter submits no intents and resyncs on resume (PC-2).

**PC-17. Save, restore and co-op seams.** `controller.state` persists (`WORLD`). **The rest normalisation lives in `snapshot()`, not in `restore()`:** `snapshot()` writes the transient input fields at their rest values — `octant = NONE`, `run = false`, `channel_ref = none`, `loco_state` as live except `CHANNELLING → IDLE` and `STAGGERED → IDLE` (stagger timer cleared); `carry_ref`, `facing`, `aim_angle` and `aim_live` as live — and `restore()` is a whole-block replace that normalises nothing, so `snapshot(restore(snapshot()))` is byte-identical in canonical bytes (kernel K:839, CR-8; Save/Load SL-15 item 10) and a loaded game still never resumes mid-move, mid-channel or mid-stagger. Integer vectors persist as two ints or a packed int array, never `Vector2i` (SL-2). This answers Save/Load OQ-9: `controller.state` is not in `REBASE_BLOCKS` (Save/Load's list, registry value `[interaction.state, assembly.state]`: `controller.state` and `body.state` are not listed, because the Controller normalises in `snapshot()` and BDY-31 makes Body's block restore-idempotent at block level); restore verification of this block is `EXACT`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* A restore restarts the run ramp (the resync re-sends `run`), so G4 fixtures walk across the snapshot tick. Body sets `previous := current` in `post_restore` and preserves `sub` (it resets on the first octant change, registry `move_octant_reset`) (pipeline C5). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from save-load-and-versioned-format, ship-body-and-interior-physics)* VS-B: a guest's Adapter submits identically and the host's Controller Sim applies; `controller.state` and `body.state` replicate as `SHARED_SUMMARY`; remote crewmates' beams rotate from the **replicated committed aim, which may not be coarsened below the facing hysteresis** (1/64 turn) so facing agrees on every client; **no shared or assisted control** of any kind; the player's **own identity glyph** (beam shape + band count) sits on the suit panel; crewmate names are **press-to-reveal for ≈ 2 s and lit-only**; pings (if adopted) mark lit things only. Nothing in the Presenter branches on `HOST` / `MIRROR` (CR-10).

*`controller.state` field table (complete).* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the published, persisted and normalised lists had drifted apart and omitted `cap_seen`, `ramp_ticks`, `last_octant` and the stagger and downed timers; this table is the single list and the two sentences above defer to it. "Rest" = the value `snapshot()` writes; `restore()` replaces the block whole and normalises nothing.)* Per controlled actor, keyed by PlayerId:

| Field | Type | Written by | Persisted value (`snapshot()`) | After restore |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `intent_octant` | int 0–8 | `set_move_intent` apply (P2) | rest: `NONE` (8) | `NONE`; the resync re-sends the hands (PC-2 iii) |
| `intent_run` | bool | `set_move_intent` apply | rest: `false` | `false`; resync |
| `octant` | int 0–8 | `controller.step` (copy of `intent_octant`) | rest: `NONE` | `NONE` |
| `gait_effective` (`v_eff`) | int 0–3 | `controller.step` | rest: 0 | 0 (recomputed next tick) |
| `ramp_ticks` | int ≥ 0 | `controller.step` | rest: 0 | 0 — a restore restarts the run ramp |
| `walk_ticks` | int ≥ 0 | `controller.step` (D.4 walk-start lever) | rest: 0 | 0 |
| `last_octant` | int 0–7 | `controller.step` (decel-ladder memory only; **not** Body's persisted `last_octant`) | live | live |
| `decel_k` | int 0–3 | `controller.step` (D.5 ladder position) | rest: 0 | 0 — a load never resumes a slide |
| `aim_angle` | int 0–4095 | `set_aim` apply | live | live |
| `aim_live` | bool | `set_aim` apply | live | live |
| `facing` | int 0–7 | `controller.step` | live | live |
| `loco_state` | enum | `controller.step` | live, except `CHANNELLING → IDLE`, `STAGGERED → IDLE` | as persisted |
| `carry_ref` | id or none | Class B mutators | live | live |
| `channel_ref` | `{owner_system, channel_id}` or none | Class B mutators | rest: none | none |
| `cap_seen` | enum {FULL, WALK_ONLY, NONE} | `controller.step` (every tick, every state) | live (equals Suit's cap at the snapshot tick) | `post_restore` re-reads the cap and sets `cap_seen := cap`; a restore never produces a cap edge |
| `stagger_end_tick` | int tick or none | `controller.step` / `stagger` apply | rest: none (STAGGERED normalised to IDLE) | none |
| `T_downed`, `death_tick` | int tick or none | `controller.step` | live while `DOWNED` (absolute ticks; the kernel tick persists), else none | as persisted; a restore into `DOWNED` keeps its remaining time |
| `chest_sim`, `hand_sim` | vec2 int | **derived** | not persisted, not hashed | recomputed in `post_restore` from `body.state` and `facing` |

Adapter-local state (`last_acked`, hold timers, run latch, device, context, pre-play tokens) is never in the block. Because only the rest-valued fields differ from live play and `snapshot(restore(snapshot()))` rewrites the same rest values, the round trip is byte-identical; `cap_seen` is the one field `post_restore` may overwrite, and it equals the cap already restored in `suit.state`, so the overwrite is a no-op for the round trip. A cap edge during `STAGGERED` neither restarts nor extends `stagger_end_tick`.

**PC-18. `LocomotionStep` test contract (Body's unit tests; listed here because this GDD defines the behaviour; → Section H).** Headless, no scene: (1) cardinal walk moves exactly 2 px/tick, `sub` stays 0; (2) diagonal walk per-axis displacement over 256 ticks is 362 ± 1 px (2 × 181/256 per tick; diagonal magnitude equals cardinal to 0.1 %); (3) run ramp: ticks 1–6 move 2 px, tick 7 moves 3; (4) stop resets `sub` to 0 and `odometer` stops; (5) x-sweep blocked by a wall at the foot box edge leaves y motion intact (slide); (6) y-sweep blocked by a 24-px wall depth from the north never enters the wall rect; (7) a closed door leaf blocks, an open one does not, in the same tick its state changes; (8) `odometer` counts post-collision pixels only (walking into a wall adds 0); (9) same command stream + same layout → identical `pos` sequence (G3 purity); (10) `post_restore` yields `prev == curr` and `sub` unchanged: snapshot mid-move with `sub != 0`, restore with the same key held, assert the per-axis cadence continues from the persisted residue *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from ship-body-and-interior-physics)*; (11) carry gait moves 1 px/tick and ignores `run`; (12) `locomotion_cap = WALK_ONLY` makes `run` a no-op without resetting `sub`.

> **Decided (orchestrator, 2026-10-26)** — *channels on UI-context entry*: entering `chart_table` or `chat` cancels **both** hold-mode and toggle-mode channels (not only hold-mode): in the co-op overlay the sim keeps running, and a channel the player cannot see must not keep burning. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `inspect_card` is excepted; the card shows the world and a channel the player can still see keeps running — PC-2 context matrix.)* *Beam while carrying*: **available**, now a user decision (below); the alternative (extraction in the dark) stays a playtest lever in Open Questions.

> **USER DECISION (2026-10-26) — chest-mounted lamp and glowsticks; supersedes every "hand point" reference above.** The player's lamp is mounted on the suit's chest, Pandorum-style, and is hands-free: the beam is available while carrying, channelling and running, its origin is `CHEST_POINT[facing][animation]` (near the centreline, so mirrors barely move it), the tool layer carries no light, and `HAND_POINT` is retained only for tool placement. PC-5's "hand point" and PC-8's beam-while-carrying question read accordingly. **Throwable glowsticks** join Suit & Equipment's deployable lights: omnidirectional, thrown on a short arc, landing as a world light the web can see; throw verb and arc are Interaction's, charges are Suit's, colour is the art-director's (inside the palette). Mouse aim is the direction from the chest to the cursor *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: measured from the facing-independent reference chest `AIM_ORIGIN` against a look-ahead-free cursor point, PC-5; the beam is still drawn from the true chest point)*.

> **Provenance of the amendments from Section D/E (adopted — decision log 2026-10-26).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: folded into the rules they amend — (1) PC-2 and PC-5, (2) D.3, D.6, PC-17, (3) D.3, (4) PC-18, (5) D.11, (6) PC-7 and Edge Cases, (7) D.7 and D.14, (8) PC-6 and D.9, (9) PC-5 `aim_release_mode` and PC-2 bindings. The list below is kept as the provenance record and overrides nothing; where it differs from a rule above (the throw binding, the pad aim default), the rule above wins.)* (1) `set_aim` carries `live: bool` and the send predicate includes `live_changed`, so a stick release reaches the sim and facing rule 4 can yield to rules 5–6; intents are `actor_binding = OWNED_BY_ISSUER` like commands. (2) Integration rounds `trunc` toward zero so W mirrors E exactly; `sub` resets on **any octant change**, not only on stop; one shared octant table (0 = E, counter-clockwise, 8 = NONE); spawn facing S; opposite keys cancel (SOCD sum); keys override the stick; `MOVE_DEADZONE_EXIT` 0.05 release band; a run press during a channel sets no latch; run-mode/latch resolution is Adapter-side. (3) Diagonal cadences are: walk 1,1,2,1,2,1,1,2…; run eight 2s then a 3 (543/256 ≈ 2.12); carry has ≈ 29 % zero-motion ticks on diagonals (flagged for the spike). (4) PC-18 test 2 is exactly 362 px with `sub = 0` at tick 256; test 4 covers the octant-change reset. (5) `odometer` is in 1/256 px with octant weights (256 cardinal, 181 diagonal) so diagonals animate at 12 fps; `frame = floor(odometer / (stride × 256)) mod 8`; a signed `anim_counter` keeps the reversed cycle continuous. (6) Channel owners' hooks declare `after: controller.step` and evaluate abort **before** accrual — a move on the completion tick aborts. (7) `STAGGER_TICKS` floor is 20 (four frames at 12 fps); `AIM_OUTER` / `AIM_CURVE` are dormant under absolute-direction aim. (8) `FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN` 0.10 (0–0.25) stops prompt flicker between near-equal candidates; ties break by smaller aim angle, then entity id. (9) **Pad default (decided):** aim follows movement until the first right-stick input, so a pad player who never aims is not stuck pointing the lamp south forever; `throw` is a separate action (KB `Q`, pad RB after the design review, ux-designer owns) submitting `throw_glowstick {aim_angle}`; `THROW_RANGE_TILES` 3 (1–5) and `THROW_ARC_TICKS` 30 (15–60) are Interaction's placeholders; the thrown light is never pre-played. **Coupling routed to the render-pipeline GDD and registry:** with `v_run = 3`, `FOLLOW_TAU ≤ 0.20` and `LOOKAHEAD_PX ≤ 75` are the operative ceilings — narrower than the registry ranges.

### States and Transitions

Locomotion states live in `controller.state.loco_state`. "Interrupts channel" means the Controller Sim clears `channel_ref` and the channel owner aborts. Contexts (`gameplay`, `chart_table`, `inspect_card`, `chat`) are Adapter-local and are not sim states; the sim state is unchanged by a context change except through the zero intent and cancel the Adapter sends on entry.

| State | Entered by | Movement | Aim / facing | Channel | Carry | Animation (art bible 5.2/5.7) |
